adoption of electric cars (ECs) has generated public discussions on a
mass scale, with consumers enthusiastically sharing their opinions over
social media, online forums, product reviews, and surveys. Sentiment
analysis or opinion mining has emerged as a widely used tool to examine
such discourse, gaining insights from consumer-created text to measure
the trends in public opinion. Earlier studies have employed sentiment
analysis in many fields, including marketing, product adoption, and
policy assessment
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22zQSwto7fux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d30ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d30ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:494ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fQ8K7I6M7ux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:494ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Withux20theux20rapidux20developmentux20ofux20informationux20technologyux2cux20theux20resultsux20obtainedux20fromux20sentimentux20analysisux20onux20aux20largeux20numberux20ofux20speechux20informationux20onux20theseux20platformsux20canux20beux20usedux20forux20commentux20classificationux2cux20productux20analysisux20andux20recommendationux2cux20consumptionux20forecastux20andux20otherux20aspectsux20ofux20theux20networkux20platformux20Withux20theux20rapidux20developmentux20ofux20informationux20technologyux2cux20theux20resultsux20obtainedux20fromux20sentimentux20analysisux20onux20aux20largeux20numberux20ofux20speechux20informationux20onux20theseux20platformsux20canux20beux20usedux20forux20commentux20classificationux2cux20productux20analysisux20andux20recommendationux2cux20consumptionux20forecastux20andux20otherux20aspectsux20ofux20theux20networkux20platformux20Sentimentux20analysisux20isux20aux20practicalux20techniqueux20whichux20hasux20becomeux20oneux20ofux20theux20mostux20activeux20researchux20fieldsux20inux20naturalux20languageux20processing.ux20Theux20traditionalux20textux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodux20consumesux20aux20lotux20ofux20humanux20resourcesux2cux20butux20theux20coverageux20ofux20artificialux20extractedux20featuresux20isux20theux20traditionalux20textux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodux20consumesux20aux20lotux20ofux20humanux20resourcesux2cux20butux20theux20coverageux20ofux20artificialux20extractedux20featuresux20isux20limitedux20andux20theux20artificialux20irrationalux20behaviorux20willux20affectux20theux20correctnessux20ofux20theux20resultsux2cux20soux20theux20traditionalux20methodux20isux20notux20universal.ux20Withux20theux20developmentux20ofux20deepux20learningux2cux20textux20pre-trainingux20languageux20modelux20andux20knowledgeux20graphux20technologyux20continueux20toux20develop.ux20Aimingux20atux20theux20researchux20ofux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodsux20forux20textux20dataux2cux20weux20summarizeux20theux20researchux20backgroundux20andux20domesticux20andux20foreignux20researchux20statusux20ofux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodsux20forux20textux20dataux2cux20andux20exploreux20theux20hotux20researchux20contentux2cux20keyux20problemsux2cux20commonlyux20usedux20experimentalux20methodsux20andux20technicalux20linesux20ofux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodsux20forux20textux20textux20data.ux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Frontiersux20inux20Computingux20andux20Intelligentux20Systemsux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.54097ux2ffcis.v3i1.6022ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux222832-6024ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux221ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fcreativecommons.orgux2flicensesux2fbyux2f4.0ux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22number:ux201ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux2242-47ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22drpress.orgux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Researchux20onux20sentimentux20analysisux20methodsux20forux20text-orientedux20dataux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fdrpress.orgux2fojsux2findex.phpux2ffcisux2farticleux2fviewux2f6022ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux223ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Dengux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yuanfeiux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222023ux22ux2c3ux2c17ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDZZqsijnT6P}{}{}The
adoption of electric cars (ECs) has generated public discussions on a
mass scale, with consumers enthusiastically sharing their opinions over
social media, online forums, product reviews, and surveys. Sentiment
analysis or opinion mining has emerged as a widely used tool to examine
such discourse, gaining insights from consumer-created text to measure
the trends in public opinion. Earlier studies have employed sentiment
analysis in many fields, including marketing, product adoption, and
policy assessment {[}30{]}. In the case of EC adoption, sentiment
analysis has been applied to examine consumers' sentiments about various
factors such as battery efficiency, charging stations, and incentives
offered by the government {[}31{]}. But problems such as regional
differences, multilingual content, and data biases due to user
generation demand the development of more robust analysis models for
higher accuracy in sentiment evaluation.

Approaches to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into
lexicon-based and machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based
sentiment analysis applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking
sentiment polarity as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used
extensively to examine opinion statements made on social media
websites\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22QqVkeuBZux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fTYM7PUUGux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22typeux22:ux22chapterux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Advancesux20inux20Communicationux20andux20Computationalux20Technologyux22ux2cux22event-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22ISBNux22:ux229789811553400ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22collection-title:ux20Lectureux20Notesux20inux20Electricalux20Engineeringux2fnDOI:ux2010.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221421-1430ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22Springerux20Natureux20Singaporeux22ux2cux22publisher-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Lexicon-Basedux20Sentimentux20Analysisux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2flink.springer.comux2f10.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux22668ux22ux2cux22editorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Huraux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Gurdeepux20Singhux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Ashutoshux20Kumarux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Siongux20Hoeux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Lauux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Kavleenux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jaspreetux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Parminderux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222021ux22ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDcn3DgHpjmT}{}{}Approaches
to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into lexicon-based and
machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based sentiment analysis
applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking sentiment polarity
as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used extensively to
examine opinion statements made on social media websites{[}32{]}. More
developed lately are machine learning methods, particularly deep models
such as transformer-based models BERT, T5, and BART, proved to be more
accurate in doing sentiment classification processes

to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into lexicon-based and
machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based sentiment analysis
applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking sentiment polarity
as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used extensively to
examine opinion statements made on social media
websites\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22QqVkeuBZux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fTYM7PUUGux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22typeux22:ux22chapterux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Advancesux20inux20Communicationux20andux20Computationalux20Technologyux22ux2cux22event-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22ISBNux22:ux229789811553400ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22collection-title:ux20Lectureux20Notesux20inux20Electricalux20Engineeringux2fnDOI:ux2010.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221421-1430ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22Springerux20Natureux20Singaporeux22ux2cux22publisher-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Lexicon-Basedux20Sentimentux20Analysisux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2flink.springer.comux2f10.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux22668ux22ux2cux22editorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Huraux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Gurdeepux20Singhux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Ashutoshux20Kumarux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Siongux20Hoeux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Lauux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Kavleenux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jaspreetux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Parminderux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222021ux22ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDcn3DgHpjmT}{}{}Approaches
to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into lexicon-based and
machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based sentiment analysis
applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking sentiment polarity
as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used extensively to
examine opinion statements made on social media websites{[}32{]}. More
developed lately are machine learning methods, particularly deep models
such as transformer-based models BERT, T5, and BART, proved to be more
accurate in doing sentiment classification processes {[}33{]}. These
models rest on the context embeddings with greater understanding of the
sentiment invariances so best fit for extracting complex EC-related
dialogues. However, most existing studies primarily address English
sentiment analysis with fewer studies on multilingual sentiment
classification, particularly for languages such as Chinese, German, and
Norwegian

l-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDa3h4MBT7Oy}{}{}Approaches
to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into lexicon-based and
machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based sentiment analysis
applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking sentiment polarity
as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used extensively to
examine opinion statements made on social media
websites\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22QqVkeuBZux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d32ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fTYM7PUUGux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:504ux2cux22typeux22:ux22chapterux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Advancesux20inux20Communicationux20andux20Computationalux20Technologyux22ux2cux22event-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22ISBNux22:ux229789811553400ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22collection-title:ux20Lectureux20Notesux20inux20Electricalux20Engineeringux2fnDOI:ux2010.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221421-1430ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22Springerux20Natureux20Singaporeux22ux2cux22publisher-placeux22:ux22Singaporeux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Lexicon-Basedux20Sentimentux20Analysisux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2flink.springer.comux2f10.1007ux2f978-981-15-5341-7_108ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux22668ux22ux2cux22editorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Huraux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Gurdeepux20Singhux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Ashutoshux20Kumarux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Siongux20Hoeux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Lauux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Kavleenux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Kourux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jaspreetux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singhux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Parminderux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222021ux22ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDcn3DgHpjmT}{}{}Approaches
to sentiment analysis are normally categorized into lexicon-based and
machine learning-based approaches. Lexicon-based sentiment analysis
applies predetermined word dictionaries for marking sentiment polarity
as positive, negative, or neutral and has been used extensively to
examine opinion statements made on social media websites{[}32{]}. More
developed lately are machine learning methods, particularly deep models
such as transformer-based models BERT, T5, and BART, proved to be more
accurate in doing sentiment classification processes {[}33{]}. These
models rest on the context embeddings with greater understanding of the
sentiment invariances so best fit for extracting complex EC-related
dialogues. However, most existing studies primarily address English
sentiment analysis with fewer studies on multilingual sentiment
classification, particularly for languages such as Chinese, German, and
Norwegian {[}34{]}.

Surveys-based sentiment analysis has also become an ancillary method to
double-check sentiment patterns discovered in web-based conversation.
While user-generated content provides real-world insights, it is not
structured and biased, leading to possible misinterpretations

\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22bxMvgOi8ux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d35ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d35ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:508ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fMM4WWB26ux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:508ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Inux20thisux20studyux2cux20weux20investigateux20newspapersux27ux20decisionux20toux20expandux20theirux20productux20linesux20byux20addingux20onlineux20editionsux20thatux20incorporateux20user-generatedux20content.ux20Weux20demonstrateux20thatux20suchux20productux20lineux20extensionsux20mitigateux20theux20extentux20ofux20slantingux20inux20printux20media.ux20Theux20resultsux20alsoux20showux20thatux20asux20theux20extentux20ofux20discretionux20ofux20usersux20toux20generateux20onlineux20contentux20increasesux2cux20printux20versionsux20ofux20newspapersux20becomeux20moreux20polarized.ux20Furthermoreux2cux20addingux20onlineux20editionsux20resultsux20inux20reducedux20profitsux20forux20newspapersux20asux20theux20additionalux20productux20variantsux20increaseux20theux20intensityux20ofux20competitionux20inux20theux20marketux20andux20theux20discretionux20awardedux20toux20usersux20limitsux20theux20abilityux20ofux20newspapersux20toux20extractux20rentsux20fromux20consumers.ux2fnux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20Dataux2cux20asux20supplementalux20materialux2cux20areux20availableux20atux20http:ux2fux2fdx.doi.orgux2f10.1287ux2fmnsc.2013.1746ux20.ux2fnux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20ux20Thisux20paperux20wasux20acceptedux20byux20J.ux20Miguelux20Villas-Boasux2cux20marketing.ux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Managementux20Scienceux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.1287ux2fmnsc.2013.1746ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux220025-1909ux2cux201526-5501ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux2212ux22ux2cux22journalAbbreviationux22:ux22Managementux20Scienceux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux222655-2666ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22User-Generatedux20Contentux20andux20Biasux20inux20Newsux20Mediaux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fpubsonline.informs.orgux2fdoiux2f10.1287ux2fmnsc.2013.1746ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2259ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Yildirimux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Pinarux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Gal-Orux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Estherux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Geylaniux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Tansevux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222013ux22ux2c12ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDN3zWoaWFDE}{}{}Surveys-based
sentiment analysis has also become an ancillary method to double-check
sentiment patterns discovered in web-based conversation. While
user-generated content provides real-world insights, it is not
structured and biased, leading to possible misinterpretations {[}35{]}.
Survey creation with real content sources offers a structured
methodology for assessing the public view of EC adoption. Models for
question generation, particularly the T5 transformer-based models, have
proven to be capable of creating meaningful survey questions{[}36{]}.
Methods such as Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency (TF-IDF) have
been applied to rank vital words that define EC adoption so that survey
responses capture congruent sentiment markers. Despite these advances,
there remains a need for more sophisticated sentiment analysis
techniques, especially with respect to the application of matrix-based
approaches to measure survey responses in a systematic way.

Machine learning algorithms have also been used to predict EC adoption
trends from sentiment analysis. Regression models such as linear
regression, K-nearest neighbors (KNN), and random forest have been used
to establish correlations between patterns of sentiment and EC sales

Informationux20Managementux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221062-7375ux2cux201533-7995ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux221ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22ngux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22http:ux2fux2fcreativecommons.orgux2flicensesux2fbyux2f3.0ux2fdeed.en_USux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221-21ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approaches:ux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approachesux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fservices.igi-global.comux2fresolvedoiux2fresolve.aspxux3fdoi=10.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2231ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Yehux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jen-Yinux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Wangux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yu-Tingux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222023ux22ux2c7ux2c31ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDQtfNZ1uyTu}{}{}Machine
learning algorithms have also been used to predict EC adoption trends
from sentiment analysis. Regression models such as linear regression,
K-nearest neighbors (KNN), and random forest have been used to establish
correlations between patterns of sentiment and EC sales {[}37{]}.
Moreover, deep learning architectures like long short-term memory (LSTM)
networks have been useful for the extraction of sequential patterns of
sentiment that are crucial in time-series forecasting

learning algorithms have also been used to predict EC adoption trends
from sentiment analysis. Regression models such as linear regression,
K-nearest neighbors (KNN), and random forest have been used to establish
correlations between patterns of sentiment and EC sales {[}37{]}.
Moreover, deep learning architectures like long short-term memory (LSTM)
networks have been useful for the extraction of sequential patterns of
sentiment that are crucial in time-series forecasting {[}38{]}. K-means
clustering has also been applied to cluster sentiment data into patterns
of consumer behavior, enabling further detailed regional examination of
EC adoption

Informationux20Managementux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221062-7375ux2cux201533-7995ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux221ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22ngux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22http:ux2fux2fcreativecommons.orgux2flicensesux2fbyux2f3.0ux2fdeed.en_USux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221-21ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approaches:ux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approachesux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fservices.igi-global.comux2fresolvedoiux2fresolve.aspxux3fdoi=10.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2231ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Yehux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jen-Yinux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Wangux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yu-Tingux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222023ux22ux2c7ux2c31ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDQtfNZ1uyTu}{}{}Machine
learning algorithms have also been used to predict EC adoption trends
from sentiment analysis. Regression models such as linear regression,
K-nearest neighbors (KNN), and random forest have been used to establish
correlations between patterns of sentiment and EC sales {[}37{]}.
Moreover, deep learning architectures like long short-term memory (LSTM)
networks have been useful for the extraction of sequential patterns of
sentiment that are crucial in time-series forecasting {[}38{]}. K-means
clustering has also been applied to cluster sentiment data into patterns
of consumer behavior, enabling further detailed regional examination of
EC adoption patterns{[}39{]}. Despite the promising outputs delivered by
such models, capturing real-time sentiments is challenging, particularly
in relating predictive analytics and dynamic sentiment dashboards.

In an efficient manner forward, the Cross-Industry Standard Process for
Data Mining (CRISP-DM) model assists in the imposition of discipline on
the methodology of sentiment analysis in EC take-up by adopting a
sequential yet systematic process that includes data acquisition,
preprocessing, attribute extraction, model building, testing, and
implementation. This results in a strict analytical plan. Past research
has established the efficacy of CRISP-DM in structuring machine learning
processes, and hence it is an appropriate methodology for large-scale
sentiment
analysis\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22Qe16l975ux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d40ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d40ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:516ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fHBZGNRDXux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:516ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Thisux20researchux20employsux20theux20CRISP-DMux20frameworkux20toux20analyzeux20sentimentux20andux20toxicityux20dynamicsux20inux20touristux20vlogux20reviewsux20thoroughly.ux20Theux20studyux20delvesux20intoux20sentimentux20classificationux20andux20toxicityux20identificationux20nuancesux20byux20leveragingux20machineux20learningux20algorithmsux20suchux20asux20k-NNux2cux20SVMux2cux20NBCux2cux20andux20DTux20withux20SMOTE.ux20Utilizingux20aux20datasetux20comprisingux20aux20substantialux20numberux20ofux20postsux2cux20theux20analysisux20revealsux20varyingux20levelsux20ofux20accuracyux20acrossux20differentux20algorithms.ux20Forux20instanceux2cux20k-NNux20andux20SVMux20showcaseux20promisingux20accuracyux20ratesux20ofux2085.90ux5cux25ux20andux2086.27ux5cux25ux20inux20sentimentux20classificationux2cux20whileux20NBCux20andux20DTux20withux20SMOTEux20yieldux2072.52ux5cux25ux20andux2071.14ux5cux25ux2cux20respectively.ux20Furthermoreux2cux20theux20researchux20elucidatesux20theux20limitationsux20ofux20toxicityux20analysisux2cux20withux20NBCux20demonstratingux20aux20precisionux20ofux2064.96ux5cux25ux20andux20DTux20exhibitingux20lowerux20recallux20rates.ux20Theseux20findingsux20highlightux20theux20importanceux20ofux20robustux20methodologiesux20forux20understandingux20sentimentux20andux20toxicityux20dynamicsux20inux20onlineux20contentux2cux20particularlyux20inux20touristux20vlogux20reviews.ux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Journalux20ofux20Computerux20Systemux20andux20Informaticsux20ux28JoSYCux29ux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.47065ux2fjosyc.v5i3.5154ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux222714-8912ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux223ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22Copyrightux20ux28cux29ux202024ux20Yerikux20Afriantoux20Singgalenux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22number:ux203ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux22648-659ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22ejurnal.seminar-id.comux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Comprehensiveux20Analysisux20ofux20Sentimentux20andux20Toxicityux20Dynamicsux20inux20Touristux20Vlogux20Reviews:ux20Aux20CRISP-DMux20Approachux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22Comprehensiveux20Analysisux20ofux20Sentimentux20andux20Toxicityux20Dynamicsux20inux20Touristux20Vlogux20Reviewsux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fejurnal.seminar-id.comux2findex.phpux2fjosycux2farticleux2fviewux2f5154ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux225ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Singgalenux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yerikux20Afriantoux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222024ux22ux2c5ux2c31ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDr8gg8ub29X}{}{}In
an efficient manner forward, the Cross-Industry Standard Process for
Data Mining (CRISP-DM) model assists in the imposition of discipline on
the methodology of sentiment analysis in EC take-up by adopting a
sequential yet systematic process that includes data acquisition,
preprocessing, attribute extraction, model building, testing, and
implementation. This results in a strict analytical plan. Past research
has established the efficacy of CRISP-DM in structuring machine learning
processes, and hence it is an appropriate methodology for large-scale
sentiment analysis{[}40{]}. With the incorporation of survey-based
validation, multilingual sentiment analysis, and machine learning-based
prediction models, this study will enhance the accuracy and
dependability of sentiment analysis in EC adoption. The findings are
expected to contribute towards a greater comprehension of consumer
attitudes and sentiments that will aid producers and policymakers in
formulating strategies to propel EC uptake globally.

Sentiment analysis, also known as opinion mining, refers to the
computational study of opinions, emotions, evaluations, and sentiments
of people. Sentiment analysis has been utilized hugely to examine how
the general public views electric vehicles by examining valuable
insights on social media, online platforms, and questionnaires. The
sentiment analysis typically comprises several key steps consisting of
data acquisition, preprocessing, sentiment categorization, and
interpretation of results.

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-26}{}{} The first step involves gathering
text data from various online sources where users express opinions about
electric cars. Data collected from various online articles mentioned in
the appendix where users discuss topics like battery performance,
charging infrastructure, and government policies. System apply web
scraping, read files and surveys to collect textual content related to
electric cars discussions. This dataset D can be represented as:

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-27}{}{}𝐷=\{𝑑\textsubscript{1},𝑑\textsubscript{2},...,𝑑\textsubscript{𝑁}\}

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-28}{}{}where each 𝑑\textsubscript{𝑖}
represents a document (e.g., tweet, forum post, or product

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-30}{}{}Given a raw text corpus T, the
preprocessing step involves tokenization, stopword removal, stemming,
and lemmatization. This can be formulated as:

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-31}{}{}𝑇 ′= 𝑓 \textsubscript{preprocess}(D)

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-32}{}{}where 𝑓 \textsubscript{preprocess}
represents the preprocessing function that cleans and structures text.

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-33}{}{}In this research we are going to
collect the user generated content from various online platforms in the
form of articles, reviews and surveys

2:ux22Procediaux20Computerux20Scienceux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.1016ux2fj.procs.2013.05.005ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221877-0509ux22ux2cux22journalAbbreviationux22:ux22Procediaux20Computerux20Scienceux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux2226-32ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22ScienceDirectux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Theux20Roleux20ofux20Textux20Pre-processingux20inux20Sentimentux20Analysisux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fwww.sciencedirect.comux2fscienceux2farticleux2fpiiux2fS1877050913001385ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2217ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Haddiux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Emmaux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Liuux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Xiaohuiux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Shiux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yongux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c20ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222013ux22ux2c1ux2c1ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDj011P5VxBi}{}{}\protect\hypertarget{anchor-33}{}{}In
this research we are going to collect the user generated content from
various online platforms in the form of articles, reviews and surveys
{[}42{]}.

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-34}{}{}Once collected, the raw text data D
undergoes preprocessing to remove noise and prepare it for analysis.
This includes:

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-35}{}{}\emph{Tokenization} Each document
𝑑\textsubscript{𝑖} is broken into individual words:

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-36}{}{}𝑑\textsubscript{𝑖}=\{𝑤\textsubscript{1},𝑤\textsubscript{2},...,𝑤\textsubscript{𝑛}\}

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-37}{}{}where 𝑤\textsubscript{𝑗 }represents a
word.

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-38}{}{}Stopword Removal

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-39}{}{}Removing common words like "the" and
"is" that do not contribute to sentiment.

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-40}{}{}Stemming \& Lemmatization

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-41}{}{}Words are reduced to their root forms
(e.g., "driving" → "drive").

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-42}{}{}Feature Representation Using TF-IDF

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-43}{}{}Text is converted into numerical form
using TF-IDF (Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency)
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22iPCfa3Q6ux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d43ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d43ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:487ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fWFNLHKHIux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:487ux2cux22typeux22:ux22articleux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Withux20theux20rapidux20growthux20ofux20Textux20sentimentux20analysisux2cux20theux20demandux20forux20automaticux20classificationux20ofux20electronicux20documentsux20hasux20increasedux20byux20leapsux20andux20bound.ux20Theux20paradigmux20ofux20textux20classificationux20orux20textux20miningux20hasux20beenux20theux20subjectux20ofux20manyux20researchux20worksux20inux20recentux20time.ux20Inux20thisux20paperux20weux20proposeux20aux20techniqueux20forux20textux20sentimentux20classificationux20usingux20termux20frequency-ux20inverseux20documentux20frequencyux20ux28TF-IDFux29ux20alongux20withux20Nextux20Wordux20Negationux20ux28NWNux29.ux20Weux20haveux20alsoux20comparedux20theux20performancesux20ofux20binaryux20bagux20ofux20wordsux20modelux2cux20TF-IDFux20modelux20andux20TF-IDFux20withux20nextux20wordux20negationux20ux28TF-IDF-NWNux29ux20modelux20forux20textux20classification.ux20Ourux20proposedux20modelux20isux20thenux20appliedux20onux20threeux20differentux20textux20miningux20algorithmsux20andux20weux20foundux20theux20Linearux20Supportux20vectorux20machineux20ux28LSVMux29ux20isux20theux20mostux20appropriateux20toux20workux20withux20ourux20proposedux20model.ux20Theux20achievedux20resultsux20showux20significantux20increaseux20inux20accuracyux20comparedux20toux20earlierux20methods.ux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.48550ux2farXiv.1806.06407ux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22arXiv:1806.06407ux20ux7bux5bux7dcsux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22numberux22:ux22arXiv:1806.06407ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22arXivux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22arXiv.orgux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Anux20Improvedux20Textux20Sentimentux20Classificationux20Modelux20Usingux20TF-IDFux20andux20Nextux20Wordux20Negationux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22http:ux2fux2farxiv.orgux2fabsux2f1806.06407ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Dasux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Bijoyanux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Chakrabortyux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Saritux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c20ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222018ux22ux2c6ux2c17ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDbFfpmOPHsC}{}{}\protect\hypertarget{anchor-43}{}{}Text
is converted into numerical form using TF-IDF (Term Frequency-Inverse
Document Frequency) {[}43{]}:

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-44}{}{}Term Frequency -- Number of time a
word occurs in a documents

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-45}{}{}TF(\textsubscript{w,d}) = Number of
times w appears in a document d / Total number of w in document d

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-46}{}{}

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-47}{}{}Inverse Document Frequency measure
the uniqueness of a word

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-48}{}{}IDF(\textsubscript{w,D}) = log (total
number of documents in the dataset D/ Number of documents containing the
word)

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-49}{}{}TF-IDF(\textsubscript{w,d})
=TF(\textsubscript{w,d}) ×DF(\textsubscript{w,D})

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-50}{}{}This helps to extract important words
from the dataset.

}∑\textsubscript{ }S\textsubscript{w}

\textbf{\emph{\textbf{}}}

Given an input sequence X, the transformer computes contextual
embeddings:

h\textsubscript{i}=Transformer(X)\textsubscript{i}

where h\textsubscript{i, }is the contextual representation of the i-th
token. These embeddings are passed through a classifier:

P\textsubscript{(y∣X)}=softmax(Wh\textsubscript{n}+b)

where W is a learned weight matrix, and b is the bias. The model
predicts sentiment labels y∈\{+1,0,−1\} corresponding to
\textbf{\textbf{positive}}, \textbf{\textbf{neutral}}, and
\textbf{\textbf{negative}} sentiments

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-64}{}{}We will identify the
\textbf{\textbf{ex}\textbf{tracted feature set}} F consists of the top k
terms with the highest TF-IDF scores ranked words by

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-65}{}{}TF-IDFF=\{f\textsubscript{1}f\textsubscript{2},...,f\textsubscript{k}\},

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-66}{}{}where k =top ranked words by TF-IDF
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22XWMOwfGRux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d46ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d46ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:524ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fZKQRUPMIux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:524ux2cux22typeux22:ux22webpageux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Theux20Impactux20ofux20Featuresux20Extractionux20onux20theux20Sentimentux20Analysisux20-ux20ScienceDirectux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fwww.sciencedirect.comux2fscienceux2farticleux2fpiiux2fS1877050919306593ux22ux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RND4vSqI8iBXP}{}{}\protect\hypertarget{anchor-66}{}{}where
k =top ranked words by TF-IDF {[}46{]}

\textbf{\textbf{}}

\textbf{\textbf{Feature Categorization}}

Extracted features F are categorized into predefined domains C:

C=\{C\textsubscript{1},C\textsubscript{2},...,C\textsubscript{n}\}

where each feature w\textsubscript{i}∈F is assigned to a category
C\textsubscript{j }based on \textbf{\textbf{semantic similarity}}
S(w\textsubscript{i,}Cj):

Category(w\textsubscript{i})=Cj argmax
S(w\textsubscript{i}C\textsubscript{j})

where S(wi,Cj) is computed using \textbf{\textbf{cosine similarity}}
between the feature vector and the category

This ensures each feature is grouped into a \textbf{\textbf{meaningful
category}} like:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\emph{\textbf{Performance}}\textbf{:}} Battery life, charging
  speed
\item
  \textbf{\emph{\textbf{Sustainability}}\textbf{:}} Emissions,
  eco-friendliness
\item
  \textbf{\emph{\textbf{Usability}}\textbf{:}} Comfort, accessibility
\item
  \textbf{\emph{\textbf{Affordability}}\textbf{:}} Cost, value
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \item
        \begin{itemize}
        \tightlist
        \item
          \protect\hypertarget{anchor-67}{}{}\emph{With Transformer}
        \end{itemize}
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{\textbf{T5-Base}} model is a
\textbf{\textbf{sequence-to-sequence (Seq2Seq) transformer}} specialized
in generating questions based on text passages. It operates as:

Q=T5(C)

where C is the context passage, and Q is the generated question. The
transformer is fine-tuned using \textbf{\textbf{Maximum Likelihood
Estimation (MLE)}}:

L(θ)=∑\textsubscript{t=1}\textsuperscript{T} log P(y
∣y\textsubscript{\textless{}t,}C; θ)

where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  P(y ∣y\textsubscript{\textless{}t,}C; θ) is the probability of
  generating token y\textsubscript{t} given previous tokens and context
  C,
\item
  θ represents model parameters.
\end{itemize}

The transformer uses an \textbf{\textbf{attention mechanism}} to focus
on key words while generating questions. The \textbf{\textbf{scaled
dot-product attention}} is computed as:

Attention(Q,K,V) = softmax ( QK\textsuperscript{T }/
sqrt(d\textsubscript{k} ))V

where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Q,K,V are the \textbf{\textbf{query}}, \textbf{\textbf{key}}, and
  \textbf{\textbf{value}} matrices,
\item
  d\textsubscript{k} is the dimension of the key vectors.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \tightlist
    \item
      \protect\hypertarget{anchor-68}{}{}Survey Response Analysis
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Survey responses are evaluated using a \textbf{\textbf{sentiment
matrix}}, where each response r\textsubscript{ij} corresponds to a
sentiment score for question q\textsubscript{i, }from respondent j:

M = {[}{[}q\textsubscript{1} r\textsubscript{11 }r\textsubscript{12}
r\textsubscript{13} .. r\textsubscript{1m}{]},{[} q\textsubscript{2}
r\textsubscript{21 }r\textsubscript{22} r\textsubscript{23} ..
r\textsubscript{2m}{]} \ldots{} {[} q\textsubscript{n}
r\textsubscript{n1 }r\textsubscript{n2} r\textsubscript{n3} ..
r\textsubscript{nm}{]}{]}

The overall sentiment for each question is computed as:

S(r\textsubscript{ij}) = \{1,0,-1\}

1 = +ve response to +ve question, -ve response to -ve question

-1 = -ve response to +ve question, +ve response to -ve question

0 otherwise

S(q\textsubscript{i})=1/m(\textsubscript{j=1}∑\textsuperscript{m }
S(r\textsubscript{ij})

where S(r\textsubscript{ij}) maps \textbf{\textbf{Likert-scale}}
responses to numerical sentiment values.

\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}

    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}

\end{enumerate}

\protect\hypertarget{anchor-70}{}{} A Regional Comparison electric cars
adoption varies regionally due to economic, regulatory, and
infrastructural differences. Three markets---Norway, China, and
Germany---exemplify this diversity.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-71}{}{}Sentiment Distribution
        Similarity
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

To compare sentiment distributions between different regions, we
represent sentiment data as \textbf{\textbf{vectors}} and compute their
\textbf{\textbf{cosine similarity
}}\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22bu9vyrAZux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d47ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d47ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:526ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fQZI4UYX5ux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:526ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Comparingux20textualux20contentux20isux20becomingux20moreux20andux20moreux20problematicux20dueux20toux20theux20factux20thatux20nowadaysux20dataux20isux20veryux20dynamic.ux20Theux20applicationux20ofux20sophisticatedux20methodsux20enablesux20usux20toux20compareux20howux20similarux20theux20documentsux20areux20toux20eachux20other.ux20Inux20ourux20researchux20weux20applyux20theux20Cosineux20Similarityux20methodux20toux20compareux20theux20similarityux20ofux20severalux20documentsux20withux20eachux20other.ux20Weux20alsoux20applyux20theux20TF-IDFux20techniqueux20whichux20enablesux20usux20toux20normalizeux20theux20results.ux20Normalizationux20ofux20theseux20resultsux20isux20necessaryux20forux20theux20factux20thatux20thereux20areux20someux20wordsux20thatux20areux20repeatedux20severalux20timesux20andux20fromux20thisux20repetitionux20isux20determinedux20theirux20importance.ux20Finallyux20weux20canux20seeux20aux20comparisonux20betweenux20theux20similarityux20ofux20documentsux20withux20normalizedux20andux20non-normalizedux20results.ux20Asux20canux20beux20seenux20inux20theux20researchux2cux20theux20normalizationux20ofux20resultsux20hasux20aux20greatux20valueux20inux20comparingux20documentsux20withux20textualux20content.ux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Internationalux20Journalux20ofux20Emergingux20Technologiesux20inux20Learningux20ux28iJETux29ux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.3991ux2fijet.v17i12.30375ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221863-0383ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux2212ux22ux2cux22journalAbbreviationux22:ux22Int.ux20J.ux20Emerg.ux20Technol.ux20Learn.ux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fcreativecommons.orgux2flicensesux2fbyux2f4.0ux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux22258-268ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Cosineux20Similarityux20ux2013ux20Aux20Computingux20Approachux20toux20Matchux20Similarityux20Betweenux20Higherux20Educationux20Programsux20andux20Jobux20Marketux20Demandsux20Basedux20onux20Maximumux20Numberux20ofux20Commonux20Wordsux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fonline-journals.orgux2findex.phpux2fi-jetux2farticleux2fviewux2f30375ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2217ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22literalux22:ux22Ylberux20Januzajux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22literalux22:ux22Artanux20Lumaux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222022ux22ux2c6ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDvthNxkq63S}{}{}To
compare sentiment distributions between different regions, we represent
sentiment data as \textbf{\textbf{vectors}} and compute their
\textbf{\textbf{cosine similarity }\textbf{{[}47{]}}}:

S(A,B)=A⋅B/∥A∥∥B∥

where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A and B are \textbf{\textbf{sentiment vectors}} for two different
  regions.
\item
  A⋅B is the \textbf{\textbf{dot product}} of the two sentiment vectors.
\item
  ∥A∥ and ∥B∥ are the \textbf{\textbf{magnitudes (lengths)}} of the
  vectors.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{\emph{\textbf{Interpretation}}\textbf{:}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  If S(A,B)=1, the sentiment distributions are
  \textbf{\textbf{identical}}.
\item
  If S(A,B)=0, the sentiment distributions are
  \textbf{\textbf{completely unrelated}}.
\item
  If S(A,B)\textless{}0, the sentiment distributions are
  \textbf{\textbf{opposite}} in nature.
\end{itemize}

For example, if Norway has a \textbf{\textbf{highly positive sentiment}}
towards EVs while Germany has a \textbf{\textbf{neutral}\textbf{
}\textbf{stance}}, the cosine similarity between their sentiment vectors
will be \textbf{\textbf{moderate (closer to 0.5)}}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-72}{}{}\emph{Clustering Sentiment}
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

\emph{}

To group regional sentiment patterns, we apply \textbf{\textbf{K-means
clustering}}. This algorithm partitions sentiment data into
\textbf{\textbf{k clusters}}, where each cluster represents a distinct
trend in sentiment
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22HtwMPGf4ux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d39ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d39ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:514ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fNSZLBVVDux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:514ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Thisux20paperux20proposesux20aux20methodux20thatux20allowsux20theux20clusteringux20andux20identificationux20ofux20similaritiesux20betweenux20usersux20ofux20aux20digitalux20tourismux20platformux2cux20throughux20theux20extractionux20ofux20theux20sentimentsux20expressedux20byux20themux20inux20theux20reviewsux20orux20commentsux20registeredux20andux20theux20subsequentux20automaticux20clusteringux20ofux20theux20usersux2cux20accordingux20toux20theux20polarityux20ofux20sentimentsux20subjectivelyux20expressedux20inux20theirux20posts.ux20Thisux20researchux20fillsux20aux20gapux20inux20theux20textux20miningux20literatureux20forux20theux20developmentux2cux20improvementux20andux2forux20reorientationux20ofux20servicesux20andux20productsux20inux20theux20fieldux20ofux20tourismux2cux20providingux20aux20methodux20toux20exploreux20theux20needsux20andux20desiresux20ofux20theux20clientux20basedux20onux20theirux20digitalux20footprintux20drawnux20fromux20postsux20andux20reviewsux20aboutux20theux20serviceux20orux20productux20inux20question.ux20Theux20sentimentux20analysisux20isux20detailedux2cux20comprehendingux20languageux20detectionux20andux20someux20specificux20languageux20syntaxux20treatmentux2cux20withux20aux20subsequentux20explanationux20ofux20theux20clusteringux20algorithmux20used.ux20Theux20developedux20algorithmux20wasux20testedux20inux20theux20userux2019sux20segmentationux20andux20sentimentux20analysisux20ofux20theirux20publicationsux20onux20aux20digitalux20tourismux20platform.ux20Theux20resultsux20obtainedux20demonstrateux20theux20efficiencyux20ofux20theux20solutionux2cux20whichux20presentsux20aux20highux20accuracyux20inux20theux20classificationux20ofux20publicationsux20inux20fourux20differentux20languagesux20andux20inux20theux20userux2019sux20segmentationux20process.ux22ux2cux22collection-titleux22:ux22Internationalux20Conferenceux20onux20ENTERpriseux20Informationux20Systemsux20ux2fux20ProjMANux20-ux20Internationalux20Conferenceux20onux20Projectux20MANagementux20ux2fux20HCistux20-ux20Internationalux20Conferenceux20onux20Healthux20andux20Socialux20Careux20Informationux20Systemsux20andux20Technologiesux202021ux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Procediaux20Computerux20Scienceux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.1016ux2fj.procs.2021.12.006ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221877-0509ux22ux2cux22journalAbbreviationux22:ux22Procediaux20Computerux20Scienceux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux22199-206ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22ScienceDirectux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Customerux20reviewsux20sentiment-basedux20analysisux20andux20clusteringux20forux20market-orientedux20tourismux20servicesux20andux20productsux20developmentux20orux20positioningux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fwww.sciencedirect.comux2fscienceux2farticleux2fpiiux2fS1877050921022298ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux22196ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Jardimux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Sandraux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Moraux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Carlosux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222022ux22ux2c1ux2c1ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDVrgoItcJf8}{}{}To
group regional sentiment patterns, we apply \textbf{\textbf{K-means
clustering}}. This algorithm partitions sentiment data into
\textbf{\textbf{k clusters}}, where each cluster represents a distinct
trend in sentiment {[}39{]}.

The clustering objective is to minimize the
\textbf{\textbf{within-cluster variance}}, formulated as:

J=
\textsubscript{=1}∑\textsuperscript{k}\textsubscript{j∈Ci}∑∥x\textsubscript{j}−μ\textsubscript{i}∥\textsuperscript{2}

where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  k is the number of clusters.
\item
  C\textsubscript{i} is the set of data points in cluster i.
\item
  x\textsubscript{j} is a sentiment data point.
\item
  μ\textsubscript{i} is the \textbf{\textbf{centroid}} (mean sentiment
  vector) of cluster i.
\item
  ∥x\textsubscript{j}−μ\textsubscript{i}∥\textsuperscript{2} represents
  the \textbf{\textbf{distance}} between a sentiment point and the
  cluster centroid.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{\textbf{Steps in K-Means Sentiment Clustering:}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\textbf{Initialize k centroids}} randomly.
\item
  \textbf{\textbf{Assign each sentiment data point}} to the nearest
  centroid.
\item
  \textbf{\textbf{Update centroids}} by computing the mean sentiment of
  all points in each cluster.
\item
  \textbf{\textbf{Repeat steps 2 and 3}} until convergence (i.e.,
  centroids stop changing).
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{\textbf{Example}\textbf{:}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  If Norway and China share \textbf{\textbf{high positive sentiment}},
  they may be clustered together.
\item
  If Germany has \textbf{\textbf{mixed sentiment}}, it may form a
  separate cluster
\end{itemize}

\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item

  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item

    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}

      \protect\hypertarget{anchor-73}{}{}Electric Car Adoption
      Prediction Using Sentiment Analysis

\end{enumerate}

Predicting \textbf{\textbf{electric car adoption}} based on sentiment
analysis involves various machine learning models that analyze user
opinions to forecast future sales

Informationux20Managementux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux221062-7375ux2cux201533-7995ux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux221ux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22ngux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22http:ux2fux2fcreativecommons.orgux2flicensesux2fbyux2f3.0ux2fdeed.en_USux22ux2cux22pageux22:ux221-21ux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approaches:ux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22Aux20Predictionux20Modelux20forux20Electricux20Vehicleux20Salesux20Usingux20Machineux20Learningux20Approachesux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fservices.igi-global.comux2fresolvedoiux2fresolve.aspxux3fdoi=10.4018ux2fJGIM.327277ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux2231ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Yehux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jen-Yinux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Wangux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yu-Tingux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222023ux22ux2c7ux2c31ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDBNop5Dlfzm}{}{}Predicting
\textbf{\textbf{electric car adoption}} based on sentiment analysis
involves various machine learning models that analyze user opinions to
forecast future sales {[}37{]}. These models include :

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-74}{}{}Linear Regression
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Linear regression establishes a direct relationship between sentiment
trends and future EV adoption:

y\textsuperscript{\^{}}=β\textsubscript{0}+ β\textsubscript{1 }
S\textsubscript{pos +}β\textsubscript{2 }S\textsubscript{neg+
}β\textsubscript{3 }S\textsubscript{neu + } ϵ

where where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  y\textsuperscript{\^{}}, represents the \textbf{\textbf{predicted
  future sales}}.
\item
  S\textsubscript{pos,}S\textsubscript{neg},S\textsubscript{neu}
  correspond to \textbf{\textbf{positive, negative, and neutral
  sentiment counts}}.
\item
  β\textsubscript{0},β\textsubscript{1},β\textsubscript{2,}β\textsubscript{3}
  are the regression coefficients representing the influence of each
  sentiment type.
\item
  ϵ is the \textbf{\textbf{error term}} accounting for variations not
  explained by the model.
\end{itemize}

The model minimizes squared error to optimize predictions.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-75}{}{}KNN Regression 
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

KNN predicts future electric cars sales by averaging the adoption rates
of the \textbf{\textbf{k-nearest sentiment patterns}}:

y\^{}=1/ki=1∑kyi

where yi represents past electric cars adoption rates from the most
similar sentiment trends. \textbf{\textbf{Euclidean distance}} ensures
predictions are based on historical patterns.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-76}{}{}Random Forest 
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Random Forest improves prediction accuracy by aggregating multiple
\textbf{\textbf{decision trees}}:

y\^{}=1/Ni=1∑Nfi(X)

where fi(X) is the prediction from the i-th decision tree. This model is
effective in capturing \textbf{\textbf{complex sentiment-adoption
relationships}}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
      \begin{enumerate}
      \def\labelenumiv{\arabic{enumiv}.}
      \tightlist
      \item
        \protect\hypertarget{anchor-77}{}{}LSTM for Trends
      \end{enumerate}
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

LSTM models \textbf{\textbf{long-term sentiment trends}} influencing
electric cars adoption. The hidden state update follows:

ht=σ(Wxxt+Whht−1+b)

where xt is the \textbf{\textbf{sentiment vector}} at time t. The
predicted adoption rate is:

yt=Woht+bo

LSTM is ideal for \textbf{\textbf{time-series forecasting}}, analyzing
how sentiment changes influence long-term adoption.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \item
    \begin{enumerate}
    \def\labelenumiii{\arabic{enumiii}.}
    \item
    \item
      \protect\hypertarget{anchor-78}{}{}Sentiment Analysis
      Visualization
    \end{enumerate}
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Sentiment visualization helps analyze public perception of
\textbf{\textbf{electric vehicle (E}\textbf{C}\textbf{) adoption}} using
statistical and clustering techniques
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22nuC6H4kKux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d48ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d48ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:528ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fELME2MNEux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:528ux2cux22typeux22:ux22article-journalux22ux2cux22container-titleux22:ux22Internationalux20Journalux20ofux20Advancedux20Computerux20Scienceux20andux20Applicationsux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.14569ux2fIJACSA.2018.090878ux22ux2cux22ISSNux22:ux2221565570ux2cux202158107Xux22ux2cux22issueux22:ux228ux22ux2cux22journalAbbreviationux22:ux22ijacsaux22ux2cux22languageux22:ux22enux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Crossrefux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Sentimentux20Analysisux2cux20Visualizationux20andux20Classificationux20ofux20Summarizedux20Newsux20Articles:ux20Aux20Novelux20Approachux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22Sentimentux20Analysisux2cux20Visualizationux20andux20Classificationux20ofux20Summarizedux20Newsux20Articlesux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22http:ux2fux2fthesai.orgux2fPublicationsux2fViewPaperux3fVolume=9ux5cux26Issue=8ux5cux26Code=ijacsaux5cux26SerialNo=78ux22ux2cux22volumeux22:ux229ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Urologinux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Siddhalingux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c2ux2c21ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222018ux22ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDu25yCziAjM}{}{}Sentiment
visualization helps analyze public perception of
\textbf{\textbf{electric vehicle (E}\textbf{C}\textbf{) adoption}} using
statistical and clustering techniques {[}48{]}. \textbf{\textbf{Pie
charts}} illustrate the proportion of \textbf{\textbf{positive,
negative, and neutral}} sentiments, calculated as:

P\textsubscript{sentiment}=(N\textsubscript{sentiment }/
N\textsubscript{total})100

where P\textsubscript{sentiment} represents the percentage of each
sentiment type, N\textsubscript{sentiment} is the count of
\textbf{\textbf{positive, negative, or neutral}} sentiments, and
N\textsubscript{total} is the total sentiment count.
\textbf{\textbf{Word clouds and frequency bar charts}} further highlight
key sentiment terms, computed using:

F(\textsubscript{w})=count(\textsubscript{w}) /
∑\textsuperscript{w′}\textsubscript{∈W}count(\textsubscript{w′})

where F(\textsubscript{w}) represents the normalized frequency of word
w, helping to identify dominant sentiment expressions shaping electric
car adoption.

To reveal patterns in sentiment data, \textbf{\textbf{K-means
clustering}} assigns words to clusters by minimizing the within-cluster
variance:

J=
\textsubscript{=1}∑\textsuperscript{k}\textsubscript{j∈Ci}∑∥x\textsubscript{j}−μ\textsubscript{i}∥\textsuperscript{2}

where k is the number of clusters, x\textsubscript{j }represents
individual word vectors, C\textsubscript{i }is the set of words in
cluster i, and μ\textsubscript{i} is the cluster centroid.
\textbf{\textbf{Principal Component Analysis (PCA)}} is applied to
reduce word vectors to two dimensions for visualization, computed as:

Z=XW

where X represents the original TF-IDF feature matrix, and W consists of
the top eigenvectors of X\textsuperscript{T}X. This allows clear
identification of \textbf{\textbf{positive and negative sentiment
clusters}} influencing EC adoption.

For predictive modeling, \textbf{\textbf{bar charts}} compare
predictions from \textbf{\textbf{Linear Regression, Random Forest, and
LSTM}}, displaying each model's output:

y\textsuperscript{\^{}}=\{y\textsuperscript{\^{}}\textsubscript{1},y\textsuperscript{\^{}}\textsubscript{2},...,y\textsuperscript{\^{}}\textsubscript{m}\}

where y\textsuperscript{\^{}}\textsubscript{i} is the predicted electric
car adoption rate from model, assessing the impact of
\textbf{\textbf{deep learning vs. traditional methods}}. These
techniques provide a \textbf{\textbf{comprehensive, data-driven
approach}} for understanding public sentiment, aiding in
\textbf{\textbf{policy-making and }\textbf{electric car}\textbf{ market
strategies}}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \protect\hypertarget{anchor-79}{}{}Fine Tuning Transformer for
    Sentiment Analysis
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT) is a
state-of-the-art language representation model for sentiment analysis.
Unlike traditional NLP models, BERT uses a bidirectional training
approach, allowing it to capture contextual dependencies from both left
and right directions in a sentence. This is achieved through a
Transformer-based architecture, which utilizes self-attention mechanisms
to understand complex linguistic relationships
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux22rKb8Jphfux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d49ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d49ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:542ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fQ9R4HF8Wux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:542ux2cux22typeux22:ux22articleux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Weux20introduceux20aux20newux20languageux20representationux20modelux20calledux20BERTux2cux20whichux20standsux20forux20Bidirectionalux20Encoderux20Representationsux20fromux20Transformers.ux20Unlikeux20recentux20languageux20representationux20modelsux2cux20BERTux20isux20designedux20toux20pre-trainux20deepux20bidirectionalux20representationsux20fromux20unlabeledux20textux20byux20jointlyux20conditioningux20onux20bothux20leftux20andux20rightux20contextux20inux20allux20layers.ux20Asux20aux20resultux2cux20theux20pre-trainedux20BERTux20modelux20canux20beux20fine-tunedux20withux20justux20oneux20additionalux20outputux20layerux20toux20createux20state-of-the-artux20modelsux20forux20aux20wideux20rangeux20ofux20tasksux2cux20suchux20asux20questionux20answeringux20andux20languageux20inferenceux2cux20withoutux20substantialux20task-specificux20architectureux20modifications.ux20BERTux20isux20conceptuallyux20simpleux20andux20empiricallyux20powerful.ux20Itux20obtainsux20newux20state-of-the-artux20resultsux20onux20elevenux20naturalux20languageux20processingux20tasksux2cux20includingux20pushingux20theux20GLUEux20scoreux20toux2080.5ux5cux25ux20ux287.7ux5cux25ux20pointux20absoluteux20improvementux29ux2cux20MultiNLIux20accuracyux20toux2086.7ux5cux25ux20ux284.6ux5cux25ux20absoluteux20improvementux29ux2cux20SQuADux20v1.1ux20questionux20answeringux20Testux20F1ux20toux2093.2ux20ux281.5ux20pointux20absoluteux20improvementux29ux20andux20SQuADux20v2.0ux20Testux20F1ux20toux2083.1ux20ux285.1ux20pointux20absoluteux20improvementux29.ux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.48550ux2farXiv.1810.04805ux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22arXiv:1810.04805ux20ux7bux5bux7dcsux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22numberux22:ux22arXiv:1810.04805ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22arXivux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22arXiv.orgux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22BERT:ux20Pre-trainingux20ofux20Deepux20Bidirectionalux20Transformersux20forux20Languageux20Understandingux22ux2cux22title-shortux22:ux22BERTux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22http:ux2fux2farxiv.orgux2fabsux2f1810.04805ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Devlinux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Jacobux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Changux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Ming-Weiux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Leeux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Kentonux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Toutanovaux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Kristinaux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c3ux2c2ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222019ux22ux2c5ux2c24ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDPq9cZ4HCXo}{}{}Bidirectional
Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT) is a state-of-the-art
language representation model for sentiment analysis. Unlike traditional
NLP models, BERT uses a bidirectional training approach, allowing it to
capture contextual dependencies from both left and right directions in a
sentence. This is achieved through a Transformer-based architecture,
which utilizes self-attention mechanisms to understand complex
linguistic relationships {[}49{]}.

Features of BERT

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Bidirectional Context Understanding: Unlike unidirectional models such
  as GPT, which process text from left to right, BERT considers both
  preceding and succeeding words, leading to a richer representation of
  language.
\item
  Pre-training with Masked Language Modeling (MLM): BERT is pre-trained
  on a large corpus using MLM, where random words are masked, and the
  model learns to predict them, enhancing contextual understanding.
\item
  Fine-Tuning for Downstream Tasks: BERT can be fine-tuned with
  task-specific datasets for applications like sentiment analysis, text
  classification, and question-answering, significantly improving
  performance compared to conventional machine learning models.
\item
  Handling Long Dependencies: The self-attention mechanism in BERT
  enables it to capture relationships between words over long sequences,
  making it effective for tasks requiring deep semantic understanding .
\end{enumerate}

Fine-tuning BERT for sentiment analysis has been widely explored across
different domains, demonstrating significant improvements in predictive
accuracy. Several studies have investigated its application and
enhancements.

Fine-tuning BERT for sentiment analysis involves adapting the
pre-trained model to domain-specific datasets, allowing it to capture
contextual nuances relevant to the given task
\protect\hypertarget{ZOTERO_ITEMux20CSL_CITATIONux20ux5cux7bux22citationIDux22:ux222MXYbCkiux22ux2cux22propertiesux22:ux5cux7bux22formattedCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d50ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22plainCitationux22:ux22ux7bux5bux7d50ux7bux5dux7dux22ux2cux22noteIndexux22:0ux5cux7dux2cux22citationItemsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22idux22:552ux2cux22urisux22:ux7bux5bux7dux22http:ux2fux2fzotero.orgux2fusersux2f9887326ux2fitemsux2fA7KZQ3PIux22ux7bux5dux7dux2cux22itemDataux22:ux5cux7bux22idux22:552ux2cux22typeux22:ux22articleux22ux2cux22abstractux22:ux22Languageux20modelux20pre-trainingux20hasux20provenux20toux20beux20usefulux20inux20learningux20universalux20languageux20representations.ux20Asux20aux20state-of-the-artux20languageux20modelux20pre-trainingux20modelux2cux20BERTux20ux28Bidirectionalux20Encoderux20Representationsux20fromux20Transformersux29ux20hasux20achievedux20amazingux20resultsux20inux20manyux20languageux20understandingux20tasks.ux20Inux20thisux20paperux2cux20weux20conductux20exhaustiveux20experimentsux20toux20investigateux20differentux20fine-tuningux20methodsux20ofux20BERTux20onux20textux20classificationux20taskux20andux20provideux20aux20generalux20solutionux20forux20BERTux20fine-tuning.ux20Finallyux2cux20theux20proposedux20solutionux20obtainsux20newux20state-of-the-artux20resultsux20onux20eightux20widely-studiedux20textux20classificationux20datasets.ux22ux2cux22DOIux22:ux2210.48550ux2fARXIV.1905.05583ux22ux2cux22licenseux22:ux22arXiv.orgux20perpetualux2cux20non-exclusiveux20licenseux22ux2cux22noteux22:ux22version:ux203ux22ux2cux22publisherux22:ux22arXivux22ux2cux22sourceux22:ux22DOI.orgux20ux28Dataciteux29ux22ux2cux22titleux22:ux22Howux20toux20Fine-Tuneux20BERTux20forux20Textux20Classificationux3fux22ux2cux22URLux22:ux22https:ux2fux2farxiv.orgux2fabsux2f1905.05583ux22ux2cux22authorux22:ux7bux5bux7dux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Sunux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Chiux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Qiuux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Xipengux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Xuux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Yigeux22ux5cux7dux2cux5cux7bux22familyux22:ux22Huangux22ux2cux22givenux22:ux22Xuanjingux22ux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22accessedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222025ux22ux2c3ux2c2ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux2cux22issuedux22:ux5cux7bux22date-partsux22:ux7bux5bux7dux7bux5bux7dux222019ux22ux7bux5dux7dux7bux5dux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux5cux7dux7bux5dux7dux2cux22schemaux22:ux22https:ux2fux2fgithub.comux2fcitation-style-languageux2fschemaux2frawux2fmasterux2fcsl-citation.jsonux22ux5cux7dux20RNDahrRlK88W6}{}{}Fine-tuning
BERT for sentiment analysis involves adapting the pre-trained model to
domain-specific datasets, allowing it to capture contextual nuances
relevant to the given task {[}50{]}. In our approach, we utilize a
labeled dataset containing electric vehicle-related sentiments
categorized as positive, negative, or neutral. The dataset is tokenized
using the BERT tokenizer, ensuring proper segmentation of words and
subwords. The tokenized inputs are then fed into the BERT model, where
the pre-trained weights serve as an initial knowledge base. During
training, the model's parameters are updated using backpropagation and
the AdamW optimizer to minimize the classification loss. This
fine-tuning process allows BERT to align its understanding with the
specific language patterns, terms, and sentiment expressions commonly
found in electric vehicle discussions.

To further optimize performance, we incorporate early stopping to
prevent overfitting and adjust hyperparameters such as learning rate and
batch size for efficient convergence. The fine-tuned model is evaluated
using standard performance metrics, including accuracy, precision,
recall, and F1-score, ensuring robust sentiment classification. By
leveraging the rich contextual embeddings of BERT, our fine-tuned model
effectively differentiates between various sentiment expressions,
enabling precise sentiment detection in electric vehicle-related
discussions. This methodology ensures that the model not only retains
the general linguistic understanding acquired during pre-training but
also adapts effectively to the specific nuances of sentiment analysis
within the electric car industry.
